# Layout plan of the Sunbeam EM7000 main controller board

This is the floorplan and the plan for the main controller board of the Sunbeam EM7000, written before any footprint is placed, as `docs/board-design-method.md` § Planning the layout asks. The board is six layers (F.Cu, In1.Cu to In4.Cu, B.Cu), 131.0 mm by 125.0 mm, outline from (1, 2) to (132, 127) in board coordinates, 1.6 mm thick, 1 oz outer copper, ENIG (JLCPCB offers HASL only below six layers), fixed at the machine's four mounting points (`docs/sunbeam-em7000/controller-board-division.md`, § The main board's floorplan).

**Coordinates.** Every coordinate here is the board editor's: x to the right, y down. The division record's datum has Y up from the cavity's lower left, so a position taken from it keeps its x and takes y = 129 − Y (its Y 2.0 to 127.0 becomes y 127 to 2, its bottom edge the board's bottom edge). The left edge is the edge the mains harness enters, the right edge the one the low-voltage harness enters.

**Stack.** Six copper layers, 1.6 mm thick (`manufacturer-limits.yaml` states that JLCPCB offers 1.6 mm at six layers). F.Cu carries the components and signal tracks. In1.Cu is the solid `GND` plane under `low voltage`, `analog sensing` and `digital`, with nothing routed on it. In2.Cu is a `GND` fill stitched to In1.Cu that carries, outside `mains`, only the signal tracks BOARD-18 permits (`+5V` and `+3V3` are tracks on F.Cu and B.Cu, not pours) and, inside `mains` only, the routes of `Mains`-class nets that have no surface path (BOARD-5, BOARD-18). In3.Cu carries signal tracks outside `mains`. In4.Cu is a `GND` fill stitched to In1.Cu that carries slow nets only, never a `Fast`, `Sense`, supply or `Mains` net. B.Cu carries tracks, a `GND` pour and 82 of the 84 test points (TEST-1, TEST-2 name the two on F.Cu), and no assembled part. **Ground neighbour, the board's own rule (a layout practice, not a mains figure or a figure of the separation basis):** every layer that carries signal tracks has a `GND` layer directly beside it on at least one side: F.Cu and In2.Cu beside In1.Cu, In3.Cu between the In2.Cu and In4.Cu fills, B.Cu beside In4.Cu. That is why In4.Cu carries slow nets only: a `Fast` or `Sense` track there would leave In3.Cu and B.Cu with no ground beside them on that side and would sit over a fill cut by other tracks. **Mains separation, how the declared figures are met (BOARD-6; the basis is unsigned at this commit):** every plane and pour on every layer stops from `Mains`-class copper by the two declared figures, 3.0 mm in a straight line in plan view and 10.0 mm along the shortest surface path that crosses no barrier slot, going round the slot ends (the six barrier slots are 1.2 mm wide, over the basis's 1.0 mm); copper may therefore stand nearer than 10.0 mm straight where a slot lengthens the path. This changes no declared figure. It replaces the single 10.0 mm straight-line pull-back, and the 10.6 mm straight-line fence the routing study used for new copper, that this paragraph carried before.

**Net names** are written without the sheet path KiCad's netlist prefixes to a label local to one sheet (`LINE_IN` for `/Mains entry and load feed/LINE_IN`). A net named in backquotes is one the netlist holds; a class named there (`Mains`, `MainsTrunk`, `MainsBranch`, `Supply5V`, `Supply3V3`, `Ground`, `Sense`, `Noisy`, `Fast`) is the board's own name, `Mains` being the only one the method fixes. `Mains` holds the 40 nets the schematic's specification lists; the other classes are the rules step's.

**Where this plan departs from the division record's block drawing** (the revision `open-items.md` owes it): the isolated converter U601 stands with its long axis across the slot between its pad rows below the module, mains row left, because the record's 5 mm strip cannot hold a 12.8 mm SOIC-20W; the digital headers J521 and J522 and the two I2C protection networks stand in a bottom-edge cell, not among the analog headers; the thermistor and sensor-input filters stand in an island against U401's converter pins; the USB port J402 and its clamps stand in a cluster at the top edge in `digital`, as the record's item 17 asks; and every test point is a pad on B.Cu. The four lid-screw holes H1 to H4, the three fiducials FID1 to FID3 and the second shunt test pads TP107 and TP108 are drawn in the schematic (456 parts in all, the net tie NT601 included) and are listed in the regions that hold them (BOARD-7, MFG-10, TEST-3); the two region rectangles (123, 2.3)–(131.7, 10) and (122.5, 116.5)–(131.7, 126.7) were added to `low voltage` and `digital` so that the holes at the right corners lie inside a region.

**Owner decisions this plan rests on or leaves open** are in `open-items.md` and the report that came with this file; they are not repeated as requirements here.

**Fill.** The courtyard demand of each region is the sum of the courtyards of the parts it lists, the courtyards of the barrier parts K201, K731, K741, U301, U601, U602, U701 and U711 counted in the region each part's courtyard stands in (not twice), and the test-point bays are added; the courtyards of the 456 parts are the real footprints' (NT601, a net tie, draws none and adds nothing to a region's demand). Each region is at or under 70 % of its rectangles, and a shelf packing of every part into its rectangles (courtyard plus 0.1 mm, decoupling capacitors and series resistors nearest their controller pins first) places all 456 parts and all 96 test pads, with the figures below.

| Region | Area (mm²) | Parts (courtyards, mm²) | Test-point bays (mm²) | Fill |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| mains | 6452 | 3933 | 179 | 64 % |
| low voltage | 2891 | 1764 | 178 | 67 % |
| analog sensing | 2560 | 1497 | 241 | 68 % |
| digital | 3478 | 1405 | 408 | 52 % |

**Test-point bays.** The 96 test pads of the plan (84 on the board now, after 12 were removed) are 1.5 mm pads on B.Cu, at least 2.5 mm from each other centre to centre and at least 1 mm from the courtyard of every part on either side (TEST-1, TEST-5), so each pad needs a 3.5 mm square clear of every courtyard, squares of neighbouring pads overlapping where the pitch is 2.5 mm. The bays below are those squares merged into rectangles, each inside the rectangles of the region that lists its test points, and their area is part of that region's fill, so it is budgeted whatever free space the front side leaves. A bay holds only test pads on B.Cu and no front-side courtyard. **Unsigned, after the island routing round r2:** the analog sensing rows above (row of the `analog sensing` region) and the digital row predate these test-point moves: TP402 now stands at (101.625, 31.85) under R302 (its 3.5 mm square (99.875, 30.1)-(103.375, 33.6) overlaps R302's front-side courtyard, a departure from TEST-5's front-side clearance for this pad, not signed), TP527 at (107.7, 19.65) in analog sensing, and TP571 at (98.8, 21.4) in the digital cell (square (97.05, 19.65)-(100.55, 23.15)). Their rectangles in the rows above are the ones of the sites before the moves.

## Plan

| Item | Applies | How the layout meets it, or why it does not apply |
|---|---|---|
| FLOOR-1 | yes | Each part's courtyard lies inside the bounds of the region whose Parts column lists it: the tab pads J101–J103 and J106–J110, the neutral shunt R105, the fuses F101, F701, F711 and F731, the relays' contact halves, the triacs T701 and T711 and the module's AC side in `mains`; the K201 coil pocket with the proof-of-life drive (U201, Q201, Q202), the module output (C301, C302, JP301), the regulator (U302, C303–C305), the relay and opto-triac stages (Q731, Q741, Q701, Q711), the heater stages (Q761, Q771), the panel stages (Q821–Q824), the gauge drive (U791) and the headers J761, J791 and J821 in `low voltage`; U601's low-voltage side with Y601, the RTD converters U620 and U621, the load-cell amplifiers U630 and U631, the sensor headers, the analogue supply filter FB301 with C408–C411 and the filter island at U401's south and east pins in `analog sensing`; and U401, Y401, Y402, the debug header J401, the USB port J402, the flow, knob and I2C inputs and the zero-crossing conditioning in `digital`. The barrier parts K201, K731, K741, U301, U601, U602, U701 and U711 are listed in both of their regions, and the rectangles of the two regions meet at the slot of each so that the union covers the whole courtyard; every connector, mounting hole and fiducial courtyard lies inside its region's rectangles. The other mains nets (the divider, converter, discharge, gate and snubber nets, each under 0.1 A) stay in `Mains` alone at the board's default track width, and no track is drawn below its rule on any of these nets. **Deviation, unsigned, for the owner to accept or refuse (T701 pin 1 and T711 pin 1, class `MainsTrunk` width 4.2 mm):** each triac's `NEUTRAL` return leaves its lead pad (pad 1, 2.2 x 1.2 mm) on F.Cu southward as a 1.2 mm wide neck, 0.57 to 1.17 mm long at T701 (two vias) and 0.57 mm long at T711 (one via), where the rule asks 4.2 mm and a 4.2 mm track can neither leave a 1.2 mm pad edge nor stand beside the gate track. The tap carries only that pump's return, 0.46 A (T701) and 0.19 A (T711) rms (`connector-pinouts.md`, Pumps: running current), at most 0.8 A at the fuse F701 and 0.5 A at F711; IPC-2221 for 35 µm external copper needs about 0.1 to 0.2 mm (0.103 mm at 0.46 A and 0.221 mm at 0.8 A, 10 °C rise), and `POWER-1`'s own 0.381 mm/A gives 0.175 mm and 0.072 mm. The 4.2 mm figure is sized for the 10.96 A trunk and the class applies it to every segment of the net. The rule and the rules file are not weakened; the pre-order check reports the necks as `track_width` findings and one `workmanship_width` finding, which this entry explains. The same finding is reported for the trunk link `NEUTRAL` from R105 pin 4 to J110 pin 1, recorded under `POWER-2`. **Deviation, unsigned, for the owner (tracks on `MainsTrunk` nets that carry no trunk current):** the stubs that join pads the 3.0 mm-clearance pours cannot reach are tracks 0.25 to 1.0 mm wide, where the class asks 4.2 mm: R101 pin 1 and R103 pin 1 (`LINE`, 0.3 mm), R104 pin 2, R102 pin 2, F731 pin 2 (`NEUTRAL`, 0.3 mm), the test pads TP105 and TP107 (`NEUTRAL_IN`), TP101 (`LINE_IN`), TP102 (`LINE_F`), TP106 and TP108 (`NEUTRAL`) at 0.25 to 0.3 mm, and the pumps' and zero-crossing detector's return link from the `NEUTRAL` copper of T701 pin 1, T711 pin 1 and U602 pin 2 to the main pour (0.8 mm on `In2.Cu`, 27.4 mm long, leaving the T711 pin 1 via at (49.15, 21.25) and running west along y = 17.85 to the main pour at x = 24.0; 0.46 A plus 0.19 A plus the detector's milliamps; IPC-2221 internal 0.5 oz needs about 0.5 mm at 0.65 A and 10 K; about 0.034 ohm at 17.5 µm copper, 14 mW at 0.65 A). The rule is not weakened; the pre-order check reports them as `track_width` and `workmanship_width` findings. **Deviation, unsigned, for the owner (parallel copper on the mains trunk, `MainsTrunk`):** the trunk runs on two layers in parallel, which takes the temperature rise from about 30 K to about 6 to 11 K at 10.96 A. Two strips are narrower than 4.2 mm and the pre-order check reports them as `track_width` findings: the `LINE_IN` B.Cu strip is 3.6 mm (4.2 mm leaves 2.34 mm creepage to `LINE` against the declared 2.6 mm ahead of the fuse) and the `NEUTRAL_IN` B.Cu strip is 2.4 mm (capped by the 1.5 mm clearance to the `SHUNT_SENSE_NEUTRAL` B.Cu diagonal); every other trunk segment meets 4.2 mm on its own layer. The `NEUTRAL` link's four vias carry 1.37 A each (barrel 0.0255 mm2; IPC-2221 internal 1.29 A at 20 K, about 1.55 A at 30 K): marginal at 20 K and inside the 30 K the trunk was designed for. The `LINE_IN` detour ratio rises because the second layer doubles its track length. **Deviation, unsigned (`Supply3V3`):** the escape of U401 pin 6 (`+3V3`) passes 0.196 mm from pad 5 (`KEY5`), 4 µm under the 0.2 mm clearance, because the 0.5 mm pitch leaves 0.35 mm from the pin axis to the neighbour's copper. |
| POWER-2 | yes | `LINE_IN` from J102 to F101's line-side clip, `LINE_F` through JP101, `LINE` to K201 pin 14 and the module feed, `LINE1` from K201 pin 13 to J101, and `NEUTRAL_IN` and `NEUTRAL` through R105 to J103, J110 and the neutral pour: straight, at 4.2 mm or wider, the neutral pour joined to every load return; the tab pads J106–J109 reach F731, F701, F711, T701, T711, K731 and K741 by the short routes of BOARD-5. `+5V` runs from JP301 to U302 pin 1, U201, U791, U630, U631 and the headers as a track 0.45 mm or wider with no neck narrower than the rest; `+3V3` runs from JP302 to U401's five VDD pins as the shortest tree. **Deviation, unsigned, for the owner (the `NEUTRAL` trunk link from R105 pin 4 to J110 pin 1):** it is two tracks 3.0 mm wide, not 4.2 mm: one on F.Cu across the 1.875 mm gap between the two large pads, where R105 pin 2's keep-off leaves room for 3.0 mm and not 4.2 mm, and one on B.Cu from J110's pad to four vias of 0.3 mm drill at (10.0, 84.3), (10.9, 84.3), (11.8, 84.3) and (11.35, 83.65) that join the F.Cu track; a fifth via does not fit (the one candidate, (10.45, 83.65), stands 1.181 mm from the `SHUNT_SENSE_IN` F.Cu tracks against 1.5 mm and closer than the existing vias). An earlier draw of this link as a pour filled to nothing (the Mains zone clearance of 3.0 mm starves a pour between pads of other nets), so R105 pin 4 was joined to nothing; it is copper now. Arithmetic at 10.96 A and IPC-2221 for 35 µm external copper (I = 0.048 ΔT^0.44 A^0.725, A in mil²): a 4.2 mm trunk track is 0.147 mm² (227.8 mil²), 74.6 A/mm², a rise of about 30 K; this link's F.Cu track is 0.105 mm² (162.8 mil²), and with the B.Cu path in parallel (about 0.375 mΩ against about 1.1 mΩ through the strip and the two vias the link then had; four vias and the inner spread now give about 0.43 mΩ) it carries about 8.2 A, 78 A/mm², a rise of about 27 K, the same as the trunk; at 10.96 A alone it would be 104 A/mm² and about 52 K. Every `LINE_IN`, `LINE_F`, `LINE`, `LINE1` and `NEUTRAL_IN` trunk segment measures 4.20 mm across along its centre line, none narrower; the tab pads J101, J102 and J103 are 1.45 mm wide (0.051 mm²) over the 1.2 mm between hole and track, about 57 mW at 10.96 A and heat-sunk by the tab. The `LINE` and `LINE_F` taps (about 1.2 mm) carry the module feed and the divider, under 0.1 A, and test pads, no trunk current. The `In2.Cu` `NEUTRAL` pour does not carry the link's current: it reaches J110 pin 1 through the barrel only. **Temperatures at commit c62b2492, unsigned, for the owner (ambient 70 °C assumed until bring-up, 35 µm copper, 10.96 A on the trunk):** two estimates are used. The resistance estimate (R = ρL/(W t), ρ at 90 °C, 0.62 mΩ per square, times I²) is the one trusted for stubs of 2 mm or less between large pads, where copper conducts the heat to both ends; IPC-2221 is a long-uniform-track figure, trusted for the 13 mm `LINE_IN` run and the 28 mm island tie, and read as an upper bound for the stubs. `LINE_IN` (4.2 mm F.Cu with the 3.6 mm B.Cu twin, 7.8 mm, 1.04 mΩ, 0.125 W): about 7 K (6.8 K on F.Cu at 5.7 A, 6.2 K on B.Cu at 4.9 A), about 77 °C. `NEUTRAL_IN` stub (4.2 mm F.Cu with the 2.4 mm B.Cu strip, about 0.19 mΩ, 23 mW): under 1 K by resistance, 28 K by IPC-2221 on the F.Cu alone as an upper bound. The `NEUTRAL` link's F.Cu track (3.0 mm by the 1.88 mm gap, 0.31 mΩ, 37 mW at the whole 10.96 A): under 1 K by resistance; IPC-2221 for it as a long strip gives 52 K at 10.96 A and 27 K at the 8.2 A it carries with the B.Cu strip and pour in parallel, an upper bound of 97 °C at 70 °C. The triac necks (T701 pin 1, 1.2 mm by 0.57 mm at 0.46 A, 0.29 mΩ; T711 the same at 0.19 A): under 0.2 K. The In2.Cu island tie (0.8 mm by 28 mm at 0.65 A, 21.7 mΩ, 9 mW): 3.6 K by IPC-2221 internal; the 0.6 mm F.Cu and B.Cu bits of `NEUTRAL` at the same 0.65 A: 1.2 K. The `MainsBranch` nets are at or above their class width everywhere, so none is a neck against its class; against the widest segment of the same net: `PUMP_C_MT2` 0.3 mm 11.9 mm at 0.46 A 1.7 K, `VALVE_COM` 0.3 mm 54.7 mm 1.7 K, `HOTVALVE` 0.25 mm 43 mm 2.3 K, so at most 2.3 K. No figure is a measured rise: bring-up measures it at full load. Widening the B.Cu strips on the same layer was tried and refused by the mains figures: `NEUTRAL_IN` at 4.2 mm stands 0.851 mm from the `SHUNT_SENSE_NEUTRAL` track against 1.5 mm (2.4 mm strip: 1.751 mm); `LINE_IN` at 4.2 mm stands 2.341 mm from the `LINE` track against the 2.6 mm creepage ahead of the fuse (2.353 mm to the `NEUTRAL_IN` pad TP107.1; 3.6 mm strip: 2.641 mm); the `NEUTRAL` link strip at 4.2 mm stands 2.217 mm from the `SHUNT_SENSE_NEUTRAL` via against 2.6 mm (3.0 mm strip: 2.817 mm), and the widest strip that keeps 2.6 mm is 3.43 mm, too small a gain to matter. Parallel layers and added vias were used instead (`POWER-3`). **Deviation, unsigned, for the owner (`+5V` at U302 pin 1):** the `+5V` track from U302 pin 1 to (70.6, 38) is 0.45 mm, except two segments: the 2.0 mm F.Cu segment (79.75, 35.55)–(79.75, 37.55) at 0.35 mm, where a `GND` via stands 0.15 mm beyond clearance at 0.45 mm, and the 4.9 mm B.Cu segment (76.5, 37)–(71.6, 37) at 0.4 mm, between the JP302 pads. The branch carries U302's input, at most its 0.45 A rating: 0.381 mm/A × 0.45 A = 0.171 mm, so 0.35 mm is twice the width the current needs and 0.4 mm more. The 13 mm neck that was 0.2 mm is 0.45 mm in the other eight segments, and the four `GND` tracks at 0.4 mm are 0.45 mm. |

Each `+5V` via is 0.75 mm / 0.3 mm; of the 23 `+3V3` vias 21 are that size and two are 0.6 mm / 0.2 mm. The current budget replaces the row's 0.2 A per via: a via carries what its barrel copper carries, and at 20 µm of plating (the class 2 average of IPC-6012; the maker's record gives no plating figure) a 0.3 mm drill via has π/4 × (0.34² − 0.30²) = 0.0201 mm² against 0.0158 mm² (0.45 mm × 0.035 mm) for the 0.45 mm track it joins, which the plan's own width rule (0.381 mm/A) rates 0.575 mm of track, 1.5 A, over the 1.055 A that the whole `+5V` rail carries at U301 and the 0.45 A (U302's rating) that any `+3V3` branch carries. The 0.2 mm drill vias have π/4 × (0.24² − 0.20²) = 0.0138 mm² against 0.0070 mm² for the 0.2 mm `Supply3V3` track, 1.04 A by the same rule. So no via is narrower than the track beside it and none limits a branch. The load of each other `+5V` branch (the sensor headers, U201, U791, U630, U631) is not recorded in `open-items.md`, whose sensor currents are unread, so each such via is bounded by the whole rail's 1.055 A and not by its own branch. `COFVALVE` and `HOTVALVE` change layer by one via each and need no more: the shared valve fuse F731 passes 0.203 A rms at inrush and 0.129 A steady (`circuit-proof.md`), the 0.46 A of `MainsBranch` being the coffee pump's. |

Effect: the longest line, 59.1 mm at about 6.1 ps/mm, is 0.36 ns against 2 to 5 ns edges, far under the critical length of about 250 mm, so it is not electrically long; a 100 Ω resistor at the far end of an assumed 80 Ω line also terminates it (reflection +11 %), so the MOSFET end is the correct damping position. The finding is cosmetic, not safety; no part is moved. The clamp is not in line with the data path. The link is full speed, 12 Mbit/s, with a rise time of 4 ns or more: a 7.1 mm stub at about 7 ps/mm is 50 ps one way, 0.1 ns there and back, 2.5 % of the rise time. Recommended: accept. The alternative is for the cleanup stage to re-site D401 in line between J402 and the vias. |

**Island routing, unsigned, for the owner (round r1c).** The `+3V3A` pads of the clamps D502 (pad 2) and D504 (pad 2) are joined: D504 by a 4.2 mm F.Cu track (diagonal to a 0.75 mm via at (112.1, 39.65) that overlaps the existing B.Cu `+3V3A` diagonal), 1.03 times the straight line to that copper; D502 by a 12.03 mm run (B.Cu around the clamp, a 0.75 mm via at (120.7, 36.025) and 1.2 mm of F.Cu into R501 pad 1), 1.44 times the straight line to R501 pad 1, above the 1.3 figure, because the ground pad D502.1 and the `NTC_COF` copper stand between the clamp and the nearer `+3V3A` copper. Every segment is 0, 45 or 90 degrees with no clearance shortfall at the class figures; the pre-order check adds two `workmanship_pad_entry` findings (D504 pad 2 is left sideways along the clamp row, D502 pad 2 turns 45 degrees 0.6 mm beyond its end) and clears nothing else. Island routing round r2 (unsigned): `VREF+` R302 pad 2 and TP402, `CURR1_AN` pin 24, `BREW_AN` pin 30, TP527, `NTC_COF`, `NTC_STM` and the clamp D506 pad 2 are now joined, with no clearance exception and none signed. Parts moved for it, all inside their region with courtyards clear: R302 0.2 mm south to (102.45, 31.75); TP402 from (99.0, 28.3) to (101.625, 31.85) on B.Cu directly under R302, its pad inside R302's courtyard on the other side; TP571 from (98.0, 35.5) to (98.8, 21.4); TP527 from (107.8, 19.6) to (107.7, 19.65); D506 0.212 mm west to (107.988, 22.2); C501 and C502 0.1 mm east to x 105.2 with their ground vias. U401 pins 15 and 16 reach the filters through vias under the body (outside every DECOUPLE-6 box) and a three-lane B.Cu street under C411 to C408 (y 27.0, 27.5, 28.0 for `NTC_COF`, `NTC_STM`, `SPARE_AN2`). The `VREF+` tap to TP402 leaves R302 pad 2 by a via south of the pad, not from pin 20's copper, so the pin 20 chain is still pin, C411, C410. Still open, unsigned: the NTC_COF clamp join D502 pad 3 (D502 stands at (123.5, 41.2) on B.Cu, 23 mm from its filter, and its only router path is a 55 mm loop). A search for a new D502 site over every analog-sensing rectangle, both board sides, all four right-angle orientations, 0.1 mm steps, with the courtyard clear of the same side's courtyards, each pad at its class clearance from other-net copper (0.2 mm Sense) and the footprint inside one analog-sensing rectangle, found no site in the island's strip (x 104.3 to 113, y 15 to 36) or south strip on B.Cu, and on F.Cu none north of y 66, so no placement shortens the join; the clamp stays where it is and `NTC_COF` pad D502.3 is the one island pad left open. D506 pad 2 reaches the `+3V3A` trunk by a B.Cu to F.Cu to B.Cu hop (vias at (108.926, 24.45) and (109.35, 25.72)). The two ground vias beside C408 and pin 19 became 0.6 mm vias at (101.75, 30.575) and (95.35, 24.85); C408.2 keeps two vias.

## Placement

In the verdict tables a line of `measures.txt` is cited without its bars (`CROSSINGS total 3332` for `CROSSINGS | total | 3332`), since a bar ends a table cell; the Measures blocks give the lines as `measures.txt` does.

### Candidate 1

**Commit**: 5295e38327f89de1073585b95405820fdb6702eb (`main-board: placement candidate 1`)

**Renders**: from the repository root, with `KICAD_CLI` set (the checkout is outside the working tree, the output folder outside every design's folder; `$SCRATCH` stands for any scratch folder that is neither):

```
git worktree add --detach $SCRATCH/placement-work/b2-wt-1 5295e38327f89de1073585b95405820fdb6702eb
(cd $SCRATCH/placement-work/b2-wt-1/hardware/sunbeam-em7000/main-board && task hw:placement -- --board main-board.kicad_pcb --project main-board.kicad_pro --out $SCRATCH/placement-work/b2-placement-out-1)
git worktree remove $SCRATCH/placement-work/b2-wt-1
```

**Measures** (four `OUTSIDE` lines name a part):

```
LENGTH | total | 9221.3971 mm
CROSSINGS | total | 3332
OUTSIDE | total | 4
OUTSIDE | R630 | listed in mains; its extent covers board x 36.77 to 44.43 mm, y 119.37 to 123.23 mm, which those regions' bounds do not hold
OUTSIDE | R631 | listed in mains; its extent covers board x 44.67 to 52.33 mm, y 120.52 to 124.38 mm, which those regions' bounds do not hold
OUTSIDE | TP103 | listed in mains; its extent covers board x 34.0013 to 35.5013 mm, y 120.5 to 122 mm, which those regions' bounds do not hold
OUTSIDE | U602 | listed in mains, low voltage; its extent covers board x 52.6 to 63.4 mm, y 120.23 to 124.77 mm, which those regions' bounds do not hold
```

Candidate 1 is the first board branch's chosen placement (its candidate 5, commit 2f172f3a) replayed on this branch's corrected floorplan, with T701 and T711 on the two-lead land. Of the 45 images of its `index.txt` (`placement-r1c1` to `placement-r9c5`), the tiles holding the parts that differ from that placement or stand differently against the corrected floorplan were opened: `placement-r2c2` and `placement-r3c2` (T711, T701 on the two-lead land), `placement-r9c1`, `placement-r9c2` and `placement-r9c3` (H1, FID2, R630, R631, TP103, U602 in the bottom-left strip). Every other figure is measured from this commit's board file, and where it repeats the first branch's candidate 5 it is because the parts it bears on stand where they stood there. ### Candidate 2

**Commit**: 78cb7f6eb36a68c5bf18d2398326e49c45f75410 (`main-board: placement candidate 2`)

**Renders**: from the repository root, with `KICAD_CLI` set (the checkout is outside the working tree, the output folder outside every design's folder; `$SCRATCH` stands for any scratch folder that is neither):

```
git worktree add --detach $SCRATCH/placement-work/b2-wt-2 78cb7f6eb36a68c5bf18d2398326e49c45f75410
(cd $SCRATCH/placement-work/b2-wt-2/hardware/sunbeam-em7000/main-board && task hw:placement -- --board main-board.kicad_pcb --project main-board.kicad_pro --out $SCRATCH/placement-work/b2-placement-out-2)
git worktree remove $SCRATCH/placement-work/b2-wt-2
```

**Measures** (no `OUTSIDE` line names a part):

```
LENGTH | total | 9159.7221 mm
CROSSINGS | total | 3345
OUTSIDE | total | 0
```

Candidate 2 is candidate 1 with the zero-crossing detector brought into `mains` and the pump column rearranged to hold it. U602 stands at (55, 23.82), turned 90 degrees, across the opto column's `mains`/`low voltage` border between U711 and U701, its mains pins facing the triacs; the steam block T711 and U711 moved up 1.6 mm (T711's tab 2.98 mm below K741 pin 13), and R805 moved to (63.2, 11.5) out of U711's way; Q701 moved to (66, 35.5) and R712 to (64, 39.5), into the `low voltage` rectangle below the column, and TP404 to (88.25, 34.5) to make room; the gate resistors R704/R705 and R714/R715 moved to the triacs' tab side, (36.2, 33.275)/(31.3, 33.275) and (36.3, 15.6)/(31.4, 15.6), turned 180 degrees, with TP702 at (36, 46.75) and TP712 at (45, 23.25); C711 moved to (38, 10.4); R630 stands at (24, 40) beside RT301 pin 1 (`LINE`), R631 at (19.5, 31.6) and TP103 at (14.5, 40.6); F711 moved 2.7 mm right, to (30.4, 24.5), so its MT2 clip stands 2.6 mm from R706; the divider R601–R603 and TP102 moved to clear F731 and TP101 by the pollution degree 2 figures; TP630 moved to (62.25, 25) beside U602's output. Of the 45 images of its `index.txt`, the tiles holding moved parts were opened: `placement-r1c2`, `placement-r1c3` (C711, R805, K741), `placement-r2c1`, `placement-r2c2`, `placement-r2c3` (R714, R715, T711, U711, U602, R631, TP712, TP630, F711), `placement-r3c1`, `placement-r3c2`, `placement-r3c3` (R631, R704, R705, T701, U701, Q701, R712, TP103, R630), `placement-r3c4` (TP404), `placement-r6c1`, `placement-r6c2` (R601–R603), `placement-r7c1` (TP102). ### Candidate 3

**Commit**: 13ec78f86679b12cd2bca49a7b71a45ad449fb7b (`main-board: placement candidate 3`)

**Renders**: from the repository root, with `KICAD_CLI` set (the checkout is outside the working tree, the output folder outside every design's folder; `$SCRATCH` stands for any scratch folder that is neither):

```
git worktree add --detach $SCRATCH/placement-work/b2-wt-3 13ec78f86679b12cd2bca49a7b71a45ad449fb7b
(cd $SCRATCH/placement-work/b2-wt-3/hardware/sunbeam-em7000/main-board && task hw:placement -- --board main-board.kicad_pcb --project main-board.kicad_pro --out $SCRATCH/placement-work/b2-placement-out-3)
git worktree remove $SCRATCH/placement-work/b2-wt-3
```

**Measures** (no `OUTSIDE` line names a part):

```
LENGTH | total | 9146.8885 mm
CROSSINGS | total | 3338
OUTSIDE | total | 0
```

Candidate 3 is candidate 2 with the U601 measurement cluster re-placed as one placement-only change, no part outside it moved: C601 (52, 87.85) and C602 (54.9, 85.9) across the AIN0 pins; the input resistors R604–R608 in a column at x 47.8 and 45.4 (R605 (47.8, 88.2), R606 (47.8, 89.8) turned 180 degrees, R607 (47.8, 86.6), R608 (47.8, 84.6), R604 (45.4, 89.0) turned -90 degrees); the supply beads F601 (51, 76.95) turned 180 degrees and F602 (48.6, 82.3) turned 90 degrees; C603 (53.85, 77.7) and C604 (55.5, 77.7), C605 (49.8, 78.45), C606 (50, 80.03), C607 (52.4, 82.35) and C608 (55.5, 82.1); and the four test pads TP605 (43.6, 77.95), TP607 (46.2, 77.95), TP608 (45.3, 80.4) and TP606 (43.7, 82.6). The point of it is routing: it is the only arrangement tried that lets the whole cluster be routed with no via in any pad. Of the 45 images of its `index.txt`, the tiles holding the moved parts were opened: `placement-r6c2`, `placement-r6c3`, `placement-r7c2`, `placement-r7c3` (the cluster, F601 and F602 against U301's courtyard and U601, the R604–R608 column, TP605–TP608). Every figure the moved parts do not bear on is candidate 2's, since no part it bears on moved; a row restated from candidate 2 keeps candidate 2's wording, a comparison with candidate 1 included. A tile is cited by its name and the board coordinates its caption states.

### Candidate 4

**Commit**: ff5fbf208c218e29680f33f230932775a8bb3bd4 (`main-board: placement candidate 4`)

**Renders**: from the repository root, with `KICAD_CLI` set (the checkout is outside the working tree, the output folder outside every design's folder; `$SCRATCH` stands for any scratch folder that is neither):

```
git worktree add --detach $SCRATCH/placement-work/b2-wt-4 ff5fbf208c218e29680f33f230932775a8bb3bd4
(cd $SCRATCH/placement-work/b2-wt-4/hardware/sunbeam-em7000/main-board && task hw:placement -- --board main-board.kicad_pcb --project main-board.kicad_pro --out $SCRATCH/placement-work/b2-placement-out-4)
git worktree remove $SCRATCH/placement-work/b2-wt-4
```

**Measures** (no `OUTSIDE` line names a part):

```
LENGTH | total | 9162.3832 mm
CROSSINGS | total | 3340
OUTSIDE | total | 0
```

Candidate 4 is candidate 3 with the net tie NT601 added and the converter's high-side ground carried as its own net, as the schematic now draws it: NT601 (NetTie_2, two 0.5 mm pads 1.0 mm apart, no courtyard) stands at (13.8, 86.65), turned 0, in `mains` beside the neutral-side terminal of the shunt, pad 1 on `NEUTRAL` touching R105 pad 4 at (11.475, 86.65) and pad 2 on `AMC_HGND` at (14.3, 86.65), 0.925 mm from that pad; U601 pin 5, F602 pad 2, C605 to C608 pad 2, R604 pad 2 and R606 pad 2 are on `AMC_HGND` and no longer on `NEUTRAL`. No other part is moved or turned, and no part list but `mains`'s changes (NT601 is appended to it in the floorplan, committed before the commit that places it). Candidate 3, its base, is candidate 2 with the U601 measurement cluster re-placed as one placement-only change, no part outside it moved: C601 (52, 87.85) and C602 (54.9, 85.9) across the AIN0 pins; the input resistors R604–R608 in a column at x 47.8 and 45.4 (R605 (47.8, 88.2), R606 (47.8, 89.8) turned 180 degrees, R607 (47.8, 86.6), R608 (47.8, 84.6), R604 (45.4, 89.0) turned -90 degrees); the supply beads F601 (51, 76.95) turned 180 degrees and F602 (48.6, 82.3) turned 90 degrees; C603 (53.85, 77.7) and C604 (55.5, 77.7), C605 (49.8, 78.45), C606 (50, 80.03), C607 (52.4, 82.35) and C608 (55.5, 82.1); and the four test pads TP605 (43.6, 77.95), TP607 (46.2, 77.95), TP608 (45.3, 80.4) and TP606 (43.7, 82.6). The point of it is routing: it is the only arrangement tried that lets the whole cluster be routed with no via in any pad. Of the 45 images of its `index.txt`, the tiles holding the moved parts were opened: `placement-r6c2`, `placement-r6c3`, `placement-r7c2`, `placement-r7c3` (the cluster, F601 and F602 against U301's courtyard and U601, the R604–R608 column, TP605–TP608). Every figure the moved parts do not bear on is candidate 2's, since no part it bears on moved; a row restated from candidate 2 keeps candidate 2's wording, a comparison with candidate 1 included. A tile is cited by its name and the board coordinates its caption states.

**Chosen**: candidate 4.

- Candidate 4 is candidate 3 with the net tie NT601 added and the converter's high-side ground carried as its own net, which the schematic now draws (R604 pad 2, R606 pad 2, U601 pin 5, F602 pad 2 and C605 to C608 pad 2 on `AMC_HGND`, tied to `NEUTRAL` at NT601 beside the shunt). Candidates 1 to 3 place the board without NT601 and with those pads on `NEUTRAL`, which is not the schematic's netlist, so none is chosen over it: a placement of the board the schematic no longer describes cannot be the placement the layout continues from.
- Candidate 4 gives every placement item the verdict candidate 3 gives it: no part is moved or turned, NT601 stands inside `mains` (`OUTSIDE | total | 0`, `MAINS-2` pass) and every other part is where candidate 3 put it. The measures move only by the net split: 9162.3832 mm and 3340 crossings against 9146.8885 mm and 3338, `/NEUTRAL` 143.7 mm with 19 crossings (181.9 mm, 25) and the new `AMC_HGND` 53.7 mm with 8 crossings, the star point being 35 mm from the cluster; `AMC_HLDO_IN` and `AMC_DCDC_OUT` and `VMEAS_DIV` one crossing fewer each, `LINE`, `LINE_F`, `LINE_IN`, `SHUNT_SENSE_IN` and `SHUNT_SENSE_NEUTRAL` one more each.
- The grounds on which candidate 3 was chosen over candidates 1 and 2 stand for candidate 4, which is candidate 3's cluster unchanged. They are the following.
- Candidate 1 leaves four parts outside the regions that list them (`OUTSIDE | total | 4`: R630, R631, TP103 and U602), so it is not chosen; for the same parts it fails `MAINS-2`.
- Candidate 2 passes `FLOOR-1` and `MAINS-2` and gives every other placement item the verdict candidate 1 gives it (the grounds are in the candidate 2 verdicts above); candidate 3 gives every placement item the same verdict as candidate 2, so the choice between them rests on the measures and on the routing of the cluster, not on a verdict that differs.
- Candidate 3 is shorter and crosses less: 9146.8885 mm against 9159.7221 mm in all and 3338 crossings against 3345, the cluster's nets shorter and crossing less (`/NEUTRAL` 25 crossings against 30, `AMC_HLDO_IN` 4 against 7, `AMC_HLDO_OUT` 2 against 6, `AMC_AIN0N` and `AMC_AIN0P` 0 against 2) except `AMC_DCDC_HGND`, 15.7 mm with 4 crossings against 13.8 mm with 3. It costs, against candidate 2: C605 and C606 stand 7.2 and 6.9 mm from U601 pin 3 against 6.3 and 3.9 mm, C601 5.7 mm from pins 9 and 10 against 3.3 mm, R605–R608 9.9 mm from their pins against 10.2, 10.4, 10.3 and 7.2 mm, F601 6.7 mm from pin 1 against 5.7 mm; one more test pad stands more than 8 mm from a pad of its net (36 against 35); the courtyards of the cluster stand nearer each other and U301's, at least 0.03 mm apart (F601, C603 and C604 to U301) against 0.23 mm, with no courtyard overlapping. It gains C608 2.2 mm from pin 4 against 7.6 mm, and the pair of each differential input nearer together (R607–R608 2.0 mm against 3.5 mm).
- Candidate 3 is chosen over candidate 2 because it is the only arrangement tried that routes the cluster with no via in any pad, which the verdicts do not see: in the scratch routing trials made on the cluster, no other arrangement tried, candidate 2's included, routed it without a via in a pad. The verdicts it fails, `DECOUPLE-1`, `SENSE-3`, `SENSE-5`, `EMI-4`, are failed by candidate 2 too, and the cluster's own figures above are the price of the routing.
- A scratch routing trial on candidate 2, at the pollution degree 2 figures and the net-pair entries of the separation basis, with a track held to the full figure from every pad of another net (a part's own pad pairs excused pad to pad only), routes both pump gate networks by hand on the two-lead land (each gate down the cut middle lead's place into its opto-triac's pin 4, each opto-triac output through one via to B.Cu and on to its gate resistor) with no shortfall, and routes the zero-crossing chain (`LINE` from RT301 pin 1 to R630 and TP103, `ZC_N1`, `ZC_LED_A` to U602 pin 1, `NEUTRAL` from T711 pin 1 to U602 pin 2) with no shortfall. Candidate 3 moves no part of those networks.

Supply order: power reaches the digital side first (+5V, U302, +3V3 into U401) and the analog supply is filtered from it, with FB301 at (97.05,31.5) feeding +3V3A_FB, then R301 and U401 pin 21 (96.5,26.2), and R302 for VREF+; no +3V3A copper leaves the module output by another route. Rerun of this item alone on the same board, with the added views: design-rules.md row `Minimum clearance` (Board Setup `min_clearance`) is 0.09 mm and cites only the multilayer 1 oz "Minimum track width and spacing" entry. The same maker record (docs/board-manufacturer-jlcpcb.md, Tracks and spacing) also states "Pad to track clearance 0.10 mm (0.09 mm locally at BGA pads)" at 1 oz and "SMD pad to pad clearance, different nets 0.15 mm", neither tied to a layer count. A board-wide clearance minimum applies to every copper pair, pad to track and pad to pad included, so 0.09 mm is smaller than the 0.10 mm and 0.15 mm the maker states for the board's 1 oz copper, and the row names neither limit. The `Pour gap` row (MFG-6) inherits the same 0.09 mm. Minor, not the basis of the fail: `Copper to board edge` is 0.3 mm while the maker's recommendation it cites is "more than 0.3 mm" (assembled boards), so 0.3 mm is not above it. Re-judged, rule half (rejudge2-RJA.md): the 0.5 mm pad-hole rule and 1.0 mm groove width are enforced by rule (6d6cf18b). |

Bearing on no critical net:

- `FLOOR-1`: Which region holds a part is decided by where the footprint stands, not by any track, arc, via or zone of a critical class.
- `FLOOR-4`: The item keeps footprints out of region rule areas through the rules file; no critical-class copper decides it, and its measured half is judged by the pre-order check that step 8 runs after the router.
- `FLOOR-5`: Where a connector's pin 1 stands against the connector table is decided by the footprint position, not by critical-class copper.
- `SENSE-1`: none of this item's critical copper is in this group's slice: the shunt R105 is read over SHUNT_SENSE_IN, SHUNT_SENSE_NEUTRAL and AMC_AIN1P/N (class Mains) and loaded by NEUTRAL_IN/NEUTRAL, and none of the Sense nets of this slice is a shunt-sense net (CURR1/CURR2 nets come from the current-sensor headers)
- `SENSE-5`: Where heat sources stand relative to the sensing parts is decided by part placement, not by critical-class copper.
- `CLOCK-4`: What the item asks, whether the rules file holds a 10 mm courtyard clearance from the crystal that exempts only the parts CLOCK-3 counts related, is decided by part courtyards and the rules file, and no copper of a critical net decides it
- `CONN-2`: Leadless package clearance from connectors, holes and edges is decided by part placement, not by critical-class copper.
- `CONN-3`: The direction a cable leaves a connector against the controller and crystal courtyards is decided by part placement, not by critical-class copper.
- `TEST-1`: Test points are dedicated B.Cu pads and their size and spacing are decided by footprints and placement, not by critical-class tracks, vias or zones.
- `TEST-2`: The side a test point stands on and the height of parts near it are decided by footprints and placement, not by critical-class copper.
- `TEST-3`: none of this item's critical copper is in this group's slice: the only low-resistance part is the shunt R105, whose test pads TP105 to TP108 sit on NEUTRAL_IN and NEUTRAL (class Mains), not on a Sense net
- `TEST-4`: none of this item's critical copper is in this group's slice: the voltage-pad tracks of R105 are on NEUTRAL_IN and NEUTRAL (class Mains), not on a Sense net
- `TEST-5`: The pad-to-courtyard clearance concerns test pads and part outlines, not critical-class tracks, vias or zones; its measured half is judged by the pre-order check that step 8 runs after the router.
- `MFG-2`: The paste openings of an exposed pad are decided by the footprint's paste layer, not by any track, arc, via or zone of a critical net.
- `MFG-7`: A designator's place on the silkscreen layer is decided by the footprint's reference text, not by any copper a net in a critical class carries.
- `MFG-8`: The reading direction of silkscreen text is decided by the legend text, not by any track, via or zone of a critical net.
- `MFG-9`: A designator's position against courtyards and parts is decided by the silkscreen text and part placement, not by critical-net copper.
- `MFG-10`: Fiducials are net-less pads set by placement, so no critical-class copper decides how many stand on a side.
- `BOARD-10`: The KEY, KNOB and TANK_LEVEL lines are in the Default class and are not routed here, and no critical-class copper decides how short their route is.
- `BOARD-11`: The distances between U301, K201, the sensing cells and U401 are part placement; no track, via or zone of a critical class decides them.
- `BOARD-12`: Iron access round the hand-fitted through-hole parts is decided by the parts and their courtyards, not by critical-class copper.
- `BOARD-13`: Part heights against the lid are decided by the parts, not by any critical-class copper.
- `BOARD-14`: The centre-to-centre pitch of the tab pads is decided by where the pads stand; no track, via or zone of a critical class decides it.
- `BOARD-15`: Room for the receptacle housings is decided by part courtyards and heights, not by critical-class copper.

### Pass 2

- **Board judged**: `398482ddff8f5168525039e68873728c75cb730d`
- **Views**: the commands the views were rendered with, as run (the placement view was rendered):

```
task hw:views -- --board hardware/sunbeam-em7000/main-board/main-board.kicad_pcb --project hardware/sunbeam-em7000/main-board/main-board.kicad_pro --class Mains --class MainsTrunk --class MainsBranch --class Mains_Entry --class Ground --class Supply5V --class Supply3V3 --class Sense --class Fast --out $SCRATCH/crit/views
task hw:placement -- --board hardware/sunbeam-em7000/main-board/main-board.kicad_pcb --project hardware/sunbeam-em7000/main-board/main-board.kicad_pro --out $SCRATCH/crit/placement
```

How this pass was judged, plainly. The review kit's shared context for this six-layer board would have been about 1.33 million tokens, too large to fork, so the check was judged by area group, by hand: six reviewers from fresh contexts, each reading the design files, the views and the placement view, and measuring the board with read-only scripts. They were A (MAINS, POWER, CONN), B (MFG, TEST, WORK), C (GROUND, DECOUPLE, EMI), D1 and D2 (two independent replicas of SENSE, CLOCK and FLOOR, one group doubled) and E (BOARD-1 to BOARD-18). The merge was by hand under the method's rule: any fail from either replica stands, a pass stands only if both pass, and otherwise the worse verdict stands. The views were rendered of the first locked-copper commit `04fdcd00`, which the copper fixes then replaced with the Board judged commit `398482dd` (33 segments out, 49 in, 4 ground vias out, 29 in, no mains-class copper changed). Every item the fixes touched was measured again on `398482dd` and the result is in its row; the other rows rest on copper the fixes did not change. Each item that failed on `04fdcd00` and is right on `398482dd` is written `pass`, with its first failure in Why, because the commit that put it right is the Board judged commit itself. Two rule-kind items the reviewers could not judge without the pre-order report, GROUND-5 and MFG-6, were measured with the repository's own board_check functions and fail on the stored fills; both are written `cannot judge, then fail`. Every `fail` below is a standing fail, left as a plain `fail` with its finding in Why and the open item or deviation that carries it; none was turned into a pass, and the history command is expected to flag each one. The critical copper is judged on its own: the rest of the copper (the router's) was not yet routed on `398482dd`, so a statement about a plane below means the plane of the critical-only board.

Bearing on no critical net:

- `BOARD-7`: bears on no critical net, because the four holes (H1 to H4 at (6.5, 122.5), (126.7, 122.5), (6.5, 5.5), (127.0, 5.5)) are non-plated and no critical-class copper lies within 4 mm of any.
- `BOARD-10`: bears on no critical net, because `KEY1` to `KEY5`, `KNOB_HOT`, `KNOB_STEAM` and `TANK_LEVEL` are in the `Default` class and have no tracks yet at this commit.
- `BOARD-12`: bears on no critical net, because it concerns iron access to hand-fitted through-hole parts, not routing.
- `BOARD-13`: bears on no critical net, because it concerns part heights, not routing.
- `BOARD-15`: bears on no critical net, because it concerns receptacle housings against part courtyards (3D clearance), not routing.
- `MFG-7`: bears on no critical net, because it concerns whether each designator is on a silkscreen layer, not any net's copper. Measured: 443 of 444 footprints have a visible reference on the silkscreen of their own side; NT601 hides its (excluded from the bill of materials, allowed).
- `MFG-8`: bears on no critical net, because silkscreen text direction does not touch a net's copper. Side note for whoever judges it: crop-U401 shows the Y401 and Y402 designators upside down, and the board file stores about 18 front-side references at 180 or 270 degrees (my read of the stored angle as absolute is not certain).
- `MFG-9`: bears on no critical net, because designator placement is silkscreen only. Side note: crop-U401 shows stacked and overlapping designators (C522/C513, R774/R800) around the controller.

## Open after the fix rounds

Rows recorded at commit `c62b2492`, after the fix rounds that followed Pass 1. Each is a fact measured on the board and a decision that is not an agent's to make: every row is unsigned, a draft for the owner, and none is an acceptance. The Pass 1 verdicts above are not changed by this section. The effect and class of each item is the assessment's (`SAFETY`, `PERFORMANCE`, `COSMETIC`), with the arithmetic in the row it came from; an item the assessment did not grade says so.

| Item | What stands on the board | Why no move was made | What closes it |
|---|---|---|---|

The shortest legal `PUMP_S_SNUB` route is 32.3 mm, no shorter than the 30.6 mm now; `VALVE_COM` has no safe shorter route (F731 is boxed in by the 2.6 mm rings, the K731 barrier strip x 46.7 to 59.7, y 37.9 to 47.5 and the 3.0 mm plan-view rule). | The owner's decision on the pump column floorplan: the zero-cross network R630 and R631, the holders F701 and F711, the gate rows R704, R705, R714 and R715, or the outline of the `NEUTRAL` `In2.Cu` pour (or a 4.2 mm link to C711 pad 2). The snubber is unfitted on the board (`BOARD-17`) and a 7 mA part. The assessment ranks it first among what matters; it gives no `SAFETY`, `PERFORMANCE` or `COSMETIC` grade to it. |

| ORIENT-1 (U601, U602, U701, U711) | The four barrier parts have pad rows that are the same size at both ends and symmetric through 180 degrees (U601 20 pads 2.10 x 0.60; U602 four pads 1.50 x 1.30; U701 and U711 six pads 1.78 x 1.52), so a part placed turned half a turn fits the pads: on U601 `AMC_HGND` (NEUTRAL by the net tie) would land on the `GND` pad, on U701 and U711 the triac gate network and the mains-side output pin would land on the LED pins that Q701 and Q711 drive (low voltage). Each footprint carries a silkscreen pin-1 dot (0.30 mm filled circle, 0.10 mm stroke, 0.40 mm across) and a chamfered pin-1 corner on `F.Fab`; none has a distinct pad 1 (shape or size). Dot against the board as read from a copy at 83c986c1: U601 dot at (57.65, 76.635), 0.35 mm from pad 1, nothing overlapping it but its centre is 0.10 mm outside the `F.Fab` body outline so it just touches the body edge; U602 dot at (49.30, 25.09), 0.35 mm from pad 1 and clear of silk and reference text, with the `ZC_LED_A` F.Cu track 0.03 mm from its edge (copper under mask, the silk prints over it); U701 dot at (61.30, 28.86) and U711 dot at (61.30, 15.26), each 0.25 mm from pad 1 but **inside the package body outline** (body half-length 4.255 mm, dot 3.3 mm from the centre), so after assembly the part hides it. | The board file was not edited in this records step. | A later board step: a pin-1 mark outside the body for U701 and U711 and, if the owner wants it, a distinct pad 1 on all four (a pad-shape change moves clearances and needs the board check re-run). Closed in the meantime by the pre-energisation orientation and isolation check in `bring-up-provision.md` (key signal row) and by confirming the assembler's rotation for these four parts against each datasheet's pin 1 before ordering. `SAFETY` if assembled wrong (the independent review's item 1). |
| MAINS-5 (T701 and T711 tab-to-lead 2.00 mm, four pairs) | Tab pad (MT2) to lead pad 1 (NEUTRAL) and to lead pad 3 (gate) is 2.000 mm edge to edge on both triacs, against the 2.6 mm creepage and the 2.0 mm terminal clearance (met exactly). The first four draft entries of `separation-basis.yaml` said F701 and F711 "interrupt a breakdown" between the pads. They do not: a breakdown across the pair carries the pump's own current, 0.46 A (coffee) and 0.19 A (steam) (`circuit-proof.md`, pump branch fuse row), which is under the fuse ratings, 0.8 A and 0.5 A, and the fuses open only at 2.1 times rating (1.68 A and 1.05 A) within 30 minutes; F101 is 10 A. The four `bounded_by` texts are corrected to say so. The same words ("the branch fuse ... interrupts a breakdown") remain in the draft entries for R704, R705, R714 and R715 (clearance, four entries) and for pin 5 of U701 and U711 (eight entries); for those the current is the gate-network current (358 V crest over 400 ohm, 0.9 A peak) and the same question applies. | Moving the lead pads is a copper change to a crowded mains area; the figure is the owner's. | The owner's decision: accept 2.0 mm on the corrected reasoning (it rests on the coat and pollution degree 2 and on the pump running on, with K201 as the stop, nothing clearing it), or move the two lead pads outward about 0.6 mm (the review's proposal, not checked here) to reach 2.6 mm; and re-word the twelve entries above if accepted. `SAFETY`-marginal. |
| MAINS-6 (R704, R705, R714, R715 pad pairs at 1.800 mm) | Each 1206 gate resistor's two pads stand 1.800 mm apart, below the 2.6 mm creepage, and the draft record holds a clearance entry for each pair (1.8 mm) and no creepage entry. Voltage across the gap: normal operation, the gate pulse only, 358 V crest over the 400 ohm pair is 0.895 A, so 179 V peak (126.6 V rms-equivalent) across one 200 ohm resistor for the microseconds before the triac latches (`bring-up-provision.md` row M14 limits it to 200 V and 1 A); opto-triac off, about nothing, because the whole gate network floats at the MT2 level and the supply stands across the opto-triac instead; **a resistor failed open** (the pulse stress is the unpublished figure of row M14), 253 V rms (358 V peak) across that resistor's gap for as long as it stays open. The record already says (`separation-basis.yaml`, comment before the net-pair entries) that the gate network nets keep the full 2.6 mm. No creepage entry was added: the open-resistor case puts mains across a 1.8 mm gap. A breakdown across an open resistor only reconnects the series circuit the resistor interrupted. | An entry at the band figure would contradict the record's own statement and the open-resistor figure approaches mains. | The owner's decision: sign creepage entries at 1.8 mm on the reconnecting-bridge reasoning above, or widen the four resistors' pad gap (a 2512 or a pair of series resistors in place of each 1206) to 2.6 mm. `SAFETY`-marginal, document gap. |
| TOUCH-1 (touch current 0.34 mA against 0.25 mA) | `circuit-proof.md` F5 compared the 0.34 mA estimate with 0.75 mA (the Class I portable row, an earthed construction) and its closure row in `open-items.md` said `met`. The limit adopted for unearthed touchable low-voltage parts (the USB shell and the operator controls, behind the supply module's Class II secondary) is 0.25 mA (row M24), and the estimate is 0.09 mA over it. F5 and the closure row now state the 0.25 mA limit and that it is not met. | The leakage is the supply module's (IRM-20-5, under 0.25 mA at 277 Vac, conditions not stated) and the estimate is arithmetic, not a measurement. | The owner's decision: a lower-leakage module, less capacitance across the barrier, isolating the USB shell, or measuring the finished unit first (row M24). `SAFETY`-marginal. |

| ENV-1 (pollution degree 2 in a kitchen, and the coat) | The record credits pollution degree 2 on the enclosure with the conformal coat as support, for a machine that makes steam and has a burst-line scenario; the creepage figures (2.6 mm and the band rows) are the degree 2 ones, and at degree 3 the list in `MAINS-4` below fails. No coating test is planned. No production record in the repository yet requires the coat: the main board's fabrication notes (criterion C17, which asks for the coat type, the maker's datasheet and the keep-outs) are not yet a file here, and the bring-up humidity step (DEL-HW-BRING-UP, criterion C17) is the only check; the heater wall board's notes (its criterion C10) are likewise not in this folder. | The coat is a manufacturing step outside the board file. | The owner's decision: treat the board as a coated, cleaned board with the coat named in the fabrication notes before it is ordered, and keep the humidity step before first mains; or hold the board to degree 3 (`MAINS-4`). `SAFETY`-marginal. |

**Owner summary.** What is left, in plain words, and what kind of matter each is (the assessment's grades; where it gave none, the line says so). The snubber distances (coffee and steam pumps, `DECOUPLE-5`, `BOARD-17`, `WORK-1`, `WORK-3`): placement-limited, owner floorplan decision, ranked first among what matters, ungraded. D503, D505, D570 and D571 clamp stubs (`CONN-1`, `BOARD-1`): placement-limited, `COSMETIC`. C401 and C406 distance (`DECOUPLE-1`): placement-limited, `PERFORMANCE` for C401 only, `COSMETIC` for the rest. C304 and C305 near the regulator tab (`BOARD-2`): part-rating change planned for the schematic, `PERFORMANCE`. R676 (`SENSE-3`): placement-limited, `PERFORMANCE`, averaged away. LSE lanes (`CLOCK-1`): placement-limited, `COSMETIC`. U401 pin 19 neck (`GROUND-4`): already minimal, `PERFORMANCE` for pin 19 only. TP607 spacing: owner signature of the drafted net-pair entry or a `TEST-1` deviation, labelled `SAFETY` by the fix round, ungraded by the assessment. Mains neck temperatures (`POWER-2`) and the `NEUTRAL` link vias (`POWER-3`): owner decision, `PERFORMANCE` for the thermal margin, `COSMETIC` for the via rule. The remaining owner items of the assessment: the plane against the optocoupler pads (`BOARD-6`), `SAFETY` by the item's own severity and physically adequate, a signed amendment or a 4.6 mm pull-back; the tab-pad pitch against `BOARD-14` and the lead length of C101 and RV101 (`BOARD-9`), `COSMETIC`; the signal via count (`BOARD-18`), `COSMETIC`, a safety item by its own severity, closing only on the owner's named acceptance; the load-cell raw lines and the reed hand box (`FLOOR-2`, `SENSE-4`, `BOARD-8`), `PERFORMANCE` for the first two, `COSMETIC` to marginal for the box; `+5V` and the load-cell lines over the plane void (`EMI-1`, `GROUND-2`), `COSMETIC`; the crystal keep-clear (`CLOCK-3`), the filter distance (`SENSE-2`), the bead order (`EMI-4`) and the gate resistors (`EMI-5`), `COSMETIC`; and the degree wording of the 10 mm creepage row (`MAINS-4`), a mains wording decision the assessment does not grade. Added from the independent safety review, all unsigned and the owner's: orientation of the four symmetric barrier parts (`ORIENT-1`), the triac tab-to-lead 2.00 mm whose fuse bound was wrong (`MAINS-5`), the gate resistor pad gaps (`MAINS-6`), touch current over its limit (`TOUCH-1`), branch fuse breaking capacity (`FUSE-1`), pollution degree 2 and the coat in a kitchen (`ENV-1`), unrouted interlock nets (`ROUTE-1`), the `ZC_LED_A` via pair (`ZCLED-1`), the `LINE_IN` to `LINE1` slot (`MAINS-7`) and the degree 3 consequences (the addition to `MAINS-4`).

Not routed, with the blocking objects: (1) `PT2_FORCE2` U621.6, `PT2_RTDINP` U621.7, `PT2_RTDINN` U621.8 and `PT2_FORCEN_SRC` U621.9 (pads on F.Cu only, x 82.7, 83.35, 84, 84.65, pad ends at y 105.35) cannot leave: the locked `+3V3` F.Cu feeder (80.05, 103.55) to (82.2, 105.7) to (83.85, 105.7) to (85.15, 107) to (85.15, 108.45) runs along y 105.7, 0.25 mm below the pad ends and 0.58 mm above C645's pad tops (y 106.38), where a 0.2 mm track needs 0.6 mm between coppers; the east is closed by the GND pad 10 and the GND vias (85.53, 105.56) and (85.705, 104.72), the north by pad 21 (0.33 mm gap), and C645's own pads are closed by the GND vias (80.64, 106.21), (81.24, 107.96) and C642 (0.64 mm gap, a track fits, a via does not). The feeder is the only copper link between the converter's supply copper (C646, C647, U621 pins 19 and 20) and the rest of `+3V3` (the copper check splits the net in two without it), so freeing the band needs the feeder re-sited, which means the Supply3V3 lock re-run: a decision for the owner, not taken here. (2) The B.Cu gap north of U621 between the `+3V3` via (83.3, 99.35) and the GND vias (84, 101.18), (85.51, 99.42) and (85.7, 100.25) passes two lanes at most (a three-lane bundle cannot leave x 83.1 by the flood check), and the lane order it forces (the north lane turns north first) puts a source west of the via on the wrong side, so only `PT2_FORCEP` uses it; `PT2_RTDINP` (source TP623.1 at (79, 104.25)), `PT2_RTDINN` and `PT2_FORCEN_SRC` (TP691.1 at (79, 111)) need three more lanes to J625.2, J625.3 and R691.1 and have none. (3) R645, R647, R648 and TP622 stand inside a B.Cu pocket closed by the PT1 lanes (the diagonals (84.53, 118) to (87.63, 121.1) and (87.5, 116.85) to (91.35, 120.7) and the runs at y 115.65, 116.65, 121.1 and 121.5), so `PT2_BIAS` (U621.1 and .2 to R645.1 and TP622.1), `PT2_FORCEP` R645.2 and R648.2 to the trunk and `PT2_FORCE2` R648.1 to U621.6 have no route on either layer. (4) J628 is closed in: the shortest B.Cu route from J628.2 to J625.4 is 134 mm round the south, the F.Cu reach from J628 ends at (114.5, 79.2) west of the J635 pads, whose two 0.8 mm pad gaps (y 77.5 and 79.5) each hold one lane, and the `+5V` F.Cu run (x 113.65 to 114.15) with the BREWA B.Cu run at x 115.4 and its via (115.4, 78.9) leave no 0.6 mm via site at the west end, so `PT2_FORCEN` J628.2 and `PT2_RTDINN` J628.1 stay open. The four converter pins need the feeder re-site, the PT2 resistors need a PT1 lane given up or a part moved out of the pocket, and J628 needs one of the BREWA or J635 runs moved; each is a placement or lock decision for the owner.

RTD front ends, round 2c-e3, PT2 finish after the approved unlocks (unsigned; the Sense class is not locked). SENSE-2 and WORK-2 for PT2 stand as follows. Moves and re-sites, each recorded here and none signed: (1) the `+3V3` feeder of the U621 supply group (C646, C647, U621 pins 19 and 20 and the U601 side copper) no longer runs along y 105.7: it now leaves the existing F.Cu stub at (80.05, 103.55) down to a 0.6 mm via at (80.05, 104.4), runs on B.Cu at x 80.05, then x 79.65 (y 105.4 to 108.35), then a diagonal to a second 0.6 mm via at (80.55, 109.25) that lands on the U620 supply diagonal; `+3V3` stays one net, the Supply3V3 width (0.2 mm) is kept and the copper lock was re-run for the Mains, Supply5V, Supply3V3 and Ground classes (7 items newly locked, 51 nets). (2) C645 moved 1.025 mm east, from (82.65, 106.85) to (83.675, 106.85), so its pads (RTDINP at x 82.9, RTDINN at x 84.45) stand under U621 pins 7 and 8 and each pin reaches its pad on a 1.0 mm straight stub; the courtyard gap to C642 is unchanged and C645 stays inside the analog sensing region. (3) Three GND vias were dropped, each pad keeping its companion via: (85.53, 105.56) beside U621 pin 10 (the via at (85.705, 104.72) stays), (79.80, 106.20) at C641 pin 2 (the via at (80.64, 106.21) stays) and (81.24, 108.94) at C642 pin 2 (the via at (81.24, 107.96) stays). (4) `BREWA_3V3_SRC` was re-sited, not re-routed: its B.Cu run at x 116.2 now has a 3.4 mm F.Cu hop between two 0.75 mm vias at (116.2, 81.4) and (116.2, 84.8), which opens a B.Cu lane pair at y 83.5 and 84.0 between them; the net is still whole. Routed: `PT2_RTDINP` U621.7 and C645.1 to J625.2 and J627.2 on B.Cu through the north gap at x 84.4 and the 45 degree lane x + y = 181.1; `PT2_RTDINN` U621.8 and C645.2 to J625.3 and, by a branch from J625.3 east at y 77.25, south on x 113.2, east on y 83.5 under J635 pin 5 and north on x 120.9, to J628.1; `PT2_FORCEN_SRC` U621.9 to R691.1 on B.Cu from the east channel (y 105.9, then x 91.45, then the 45 degree lane to a via at (104.1, 86.625)); `PT2_FORCEN` J625.4 to J628.2 on B.Cu (x 110.525 south, then y 84.0 east, then north into the pad); `PT2_FORCEP` re-laid in the north gap at x 84.0 (west of RTDINP at x 84.4), the same source via and the same J625.1 and J627.1 ends. The converter escapes use no via in a pad: pins 7 and 8 reach the C645 pads direct, the RTDINP via is at (81.6, 106.85) west of pad 1, the RTDINN via at (85.7, 106.7) east of pad 2 and the FORCEN_SRC via at (85.7, 105.9); every via is 0.6/0.2 and beside its pad; RTDINN and FORCEN_SRC leave the converter side by side on the east channel (0.8 mm apart at the vias, bundled to 0.4 mm at x 91.45 and 91.85) through the 1.68 mm gap between the GND vias (90.57, 104.1) and (93.0, 103.36). No lane uses a J635 pad gap. 0 clearance shortfalls, 0 off-angle, 0 short segments by the exact check at every step; the independent net check reports 0 pad-net mismatches, the open pads 309 to 302 and the Sense split 18/31 to 16/24, `+3V3`, `GND`-class pads and the BREWA nets whole, and no net newly split. Detour above 1.3, copper length against the straight line between the two pads: `PT2_RTDINN` U621.8 to J625.3 1.32, U621.8 to J628.1 1.57, `PT2_FORCEN` J625.4 to J628.2 1.47 and R691.2 to J628.2 1.52 (the J628 pair has to pass under J635 and round the J625 pad column; `PT2_RTDINP` 1.26 and 1.27, `PT2_FORCEN_SRC` 1.21 and `PT2_FORCEP` 1.08 to J625.1 are under the figure). Still open, with the blocking objects: `PT2_BIAS` (U621.1 and .2, R645.1, TP622.1), `PT2_FORCE2` (U621.6 to R647.1 and R648.1), `PT2_FORCEP` south (R645.2, R648.2), `PT2_RTDINP` TP623.1 and `PT2_FORCEN_SRC` TP691.1. U621 pin 6 can only leave west on F.Cu along y 105.65 and the two BIAS pins only by a via at about (80.95, 100.5), and all of these then have to go south: the only B.Cu way is the column x 77.7 to 78.2, 0.6 mm wide between the PT1_BIAS via (77.0, 106.88) and the B.Cu test pads TP623 (79, 104.25) and TP691 (79, 111) (pad edges at x 78.5), which holds two lanes at most, with the new feeder lane at x 79.65 closing its east side from y 104.4 to 109.25; three nets need three lanes, the two test pads need a tap across them, and R645, R647 and R648 with TP622 stand in the pocket under the PT1 lanes (y 115.65 to 121.5) that only that column reaches. A GND via at (80.64, 106.21) would also have to be re-sited for pin 6. The smallest extra lever is to move TP623 and TP691 (test pads, one net each, not part of the approval) out of x 78.5 to 79.5, which opens a 1.7 mm column for three lanes and lets each pad be tapped from its own lane; its blast radius is the two pads and the lane order in x 76.8 to 80, nothing on a locked class. The J628 pair no longer needs a lane through a J635 pad gap, so the load-cell routes keep both gaps and the open B.Cu west of J635 between y 77 and 82 now that the x 116.2 wall is hopped; the PT2 branch at y 83.5 and 84.0 lies south of J635 pin 5.

RTD front ends, round 2c-e4, PT2 finish (unsigned; the Sense class is not locked). The five items left open at round 2c-e3 are routed. Test points re-sited, each still on its own net, on the B side, with a 1.0 mm tap to its net's lane: `TP623` (RTDINP) from (79, 104.25) to (81.15, 104.9), tapped to the B.Cu RTDINP lane at x 82.05; `TP691` (FORCEN_SRC) from (79, 111) to (88.5, 104.8), tapped to the FORCEN_SRC lane at y 105.9; `TP622` (BIAS) from (84, 120) to (83.4, 120) so that the BIAS via at (84.5, 120) clears the pad. Three 0.2 mm B.Cu lanes now run south in the freed west column and turn east in the strip south of the PT1 lanes, nested without crossing (west to east in the column below y 111: `PT2_BIAS` x 77.3, `PT2_FORCE2` x 77.7, `PT2_FORCEP` x 78.2). `PT2_BIAS` leaves U621 pins 1 and 2 (joined on F.Cu at x 81.64) through a 0.6 mm via at (80.95, 100.5), runs west on B.Cu y 100.5, down x 78.6 and the column to y 126.0, east to x 84.8, then rises through a via at (84.8, 123.7) to an F.Cu run north at x 84.5 to R645.1, with a via at (84.5, 120) for `TP622`. `PT2_FORCE2` leaves pin 6 on F.Cu along y 105.3 (clear of the GND via at (80.64, 106.21), so that via stays) to a via at (78.35, 105.3), south in the column and east on y 122.75 to a via at (85.4, 123.125) beside R648.1. `PT2_FORCEP` south runs from the existing via at (80.75, 103.15) west on y 103.15, down the column and east on y 122.15 to a via at (85.4, 122.15) beside R648.2, which the existing F.Cu stub joins to R645.2. No GND or +3V3 object moved. The exact clearance check reports 0 shortfalls, 0 off-angle and 0 short segments; the independent net check reports 0 pad-net mismatches, the open pads 302 to 295 and the Sense split 16/24 to 11/17, all five PT2 nets whole, no net newly split. Detour above 1.3, copper length against the straight line between the two pads: `PT2_BIAS` U621.1 to R645.1 about 2.6 (the way into the pocket is the long west column and the south strip, the pocket being closed on B.Cu by the PT1 lanes at y 120.7 to 121.5), `PT2_FORCE2` U621.6 to R648.1 1.69 and to R647.1 1.57, `PT2_FORCEP` south V3 to R648.2 1.57 and to R645.2 1.69. The test point courtyards of `TP623` and `TP691` overlap F.Cu parts (U621, C675) on the opposite side only, as `TP503` and `TP571` already do.

Load-cell amplifiers, round 2c-e5, local nets (unsigned; the Sense class is not locked). `WT1_INA_N`, `WT1_INA_P`, `WT1_VBG`, `WT2_INA_N` and `WT2_INA_P` are routed pad to pad on F.Cu, with no clearance shortfall and every segment at 0, 45 or 90 degrees. Test points re-sited: `TP671` (WT1_INA_P) from (102, 101.5) to (87.9, 123.2) on the B side, tapped through a 0.6 mm via at (88.8, 122.3) beside `R670.2`, because the B.Cu `PT1_RTDINN`, `PT1_RTDINP` and `PT1_FORCEP` lanes close every other pocket near that net; `TP673` (WT2_INA_P) stays and is tapped through a via at (87.75, 107.5) from the F.Cu lane at x 88.4. `WT2_INA_N` joins `R676.2` (east of `U631`) to `U631.7` through two 0.6 mm vias, at (93.2, 107.17) and (100.5, 106.98), and a B.Cu run along y 106.02, because the +5V trunk at x 94.05 closes the channel between the pin columns on F.Cu. Detours above 1.3 times the straight line: `WT1_VBG` U630.6 to C672.1 1.38, `WT2_INA_N` C675.2 to U631.7 1.39, `WT2_INA_P` C675.1 to R675.2 1.40.

Load-cell amplifiers, round 2c-e6, WT1 raw lines (unsigned; the Sense class is not locked). `WT1_SP_RAW` (J630.4 to R670.1) and `WT1_SN_RAW` (J630.3 to R671.1) are routed on F.Cu with no via, no clearance shortfall and every segment at 0, 45 or 90 degrees. Both leave the header to the east, clear of the B.Cu PT1 and PT2 lane wall at x 108 to 112, and run round the south of the J521 and J522 connectors. They cannot run adjacent: the strip between the connector pads and the Ground via at (101.5, 124.62) takes one track at y 121.7 and one at y 125.35, and no more. Detours above 1.3 times the straight line: `WT1_SP_RAW` 47.2 mm for 30.1 mm (1.57) and `WT1_SN_RAW` 65.3 mm for 27.7 mm (2.36). The two lines differ in length by 18 mm, above the 5 mm aim; bring-up checks the bridge reading noise.

Load-cell amplifiers, round 2c-e6, WT2 raw lines and WT2_VBG (unsigned; the Sense class is not locked). `WT2_SN_RAW` (J635.3 to R676.1) and `WT2_SP_RAW` (J635.4 to R675.1) each take two 0.6 mm vias beside a pad (SN at (114.8, 84.65) and (100.1, 103.88), SP at (116.95, 82.1) and (90, 107.93)) and run on B.Cu between them; `WT2_VBG` (U631.6 to C677.1) takes two (at (93.15, 105.91) and (102.8, 105.56)) and runs on B.Cu between them. No clearance shortfall, every segment at 0, 45 or 90 degrees. The two raw lines are not adjacent: routed one after the other, the second found no path beside the first. Detours above 1.3 times the straight line: `WT2_SP_RAW` 61.8 mm for 39.6 mm (1.56). `WT2_SN_RAW` is 38.1 mm for 33.4 mm (1.14), so the pair differs by 23.7 mm in length, above the 5 mm aim. `C677` stays where it stands, 17.5 mm from `U631.6` (1.05 times the straight line); the VBG capacitor is far from its pin, so bring-up checks the reading noise on both load-cell channels.

Fast class, round 2d, USB and AMC local nets (unsigned; the Fast class is not locked). Routed and whole by the independent net check: `USB_DP` (J402.1, D401.1 and .6, U401.71, TP411.1) and `USB_DM` (J402.2, D401.3 and .4, U401.70, TP412.1). Both run as one adjacent pair on B.Cu, 0.4 mm pitch, from the J402 through-hole pads, with D401 tapped by a 0.6 mm via on the bridge between its two pads (`USB_DP` on the y 7.25 bridge, `USB_DM` by an F.Cu spur from a via at (72.2, 6.2) that crosses over the `USB_DP` run), a 45 degree descent at x 77.4 to 83.9 to the band south of the GND via at (87, 12.07), and a 0.6 mm via beside U401 pins 71 and 70 with an F.Cu stub up to the pad; the two B.Cu lanes enter the pin vias at (88.5, 14.35) and (89.9, 14.6). Three vias each, copper length to the pin 28.84 mm (`USB_DP`) and 29.04 mm (`USB_DM`), 0.2 mm apart. `USB_DP` reaches TP411.1 by an F.Cu stub north of pin 71 to a via at (88.5, 8.9) and a B.Cu run to (94.5, 4.75). Detour above 1.3, copper length against the straight line between the two pads: `USB_DP` J402.1 to U401.71 1.38 and `USB_DM` J402.2 to U401.70 1.53; the cause is the same for both, the B.Cu band between x 81 and 85.5 and y 5.5 to 9.5 is closed by the +3V3 diagonal and the row of GND vias at y 6.2, and the F.Cu corridor is closed by R798 and F401, so the pair has to drop to the band at y 13 south of the pin row. `AMC_CLK_OUT` (R609.1 to Y601.3) is whole at 1.05. `AMC_CLKIN` (U601.17, R609.2, TP602.1) is whole: a 0.6 mm via at (68.4, 81.3) beside pin 17, a B.Cu run at y 80.65 and a spur to a via at (71.22, 82.7) over R609.2; U601.17 to R609.2 1.58 (the F.Cu way east of pin 17 is closed by the C612 ground neck and the GND vias at (70, 81.55) and (70.33, 82.3)). `AMC_DOUT_PIN` (U601.12 to R613.1) is whole at 1.8 because the RESET pad U601.11 reaches x 68.4 and the track must pass it at x 68.75. Open, with the blocking objects. The crystals: all four MCU pins U401.8, .9, .12 and .13 leave only north under the package, since the locked +3V3 run (the diagonal from (91.5, 26.18) to (92.5, 28.05) and the horizontal at y 28.0 to 28.05 from x 87.75 to 94.45) closes the south side 0.8 mm from the pad ends; U401.9 (`LSE_OUT`) cannot leave at all, because the corner of pad 8 at (90.15, 25.38) and the locked GND via at (91.0, 24.925) leave a 0.589 mm passage where a 0.2 mm track needs 0.6 mm; the smallest lever is to move that GND via (and its 0.28 mm stub) 0.3 mm east or north, a Ground copper-lock re-run. The pins of each crystal are in the reverse order of its pads (the IN pin lies west of the OUT pin and the IN pad lies east of the OUT pad), so each crystal needs one crossing, which only a second layer can give, and the B.Cu run at x 89.9 (`+5V_GAUGE`, locked) divides the area under the crystals, so each crystal net needs a via at the MCU, a via beside each crystal and load-capacitor pad (C414.1 only from the west at (90.9, 32.675), Y401.1 only from the east pocket at about (93.95, 28.95), Y402.1 from the channel at (85.45, 28.65), Y402.2 from (83.95, 27.0), C416.1 only from the west), and the two crystals' crossings meet the same B.Cu band; the smallest lever for the pair is to re-site the +3V3 legs at x 87.3, x 91.05, x 84.8 and y 29.95 and the diagonals around the crystals, a Supply3V3 lock re-run, after which each crystal needs one crossing hop. `HSE_IN`, `HSE_OUT`, `LSE_IN`, `LSE_OUT` stay open (9 pads). SPI2 and the AMC clock and data lines: `SPI2_SCK`, `SPI2_MOSI`, `SPI2_MISO` (U401.52 to .54 and U620 and U621 pins 11, 12 and 14, 10 pads) and `AMC_SCLK`, `AMC_DIN` (R610.2 and R611.2 to U601.18 and .20) are not routed: the converter end has no exit, since the U621 and U620 pads on x 86.36 are walled on the east by GND vias at (87.07, 100.98), (88.45, 102.17), (89.75, 102.17) and (90.31, 103.12), the WT2_INA_P run at x 88.4 and the F.Cu parts of U630 and U631, leaving only an F.Cu lane at x 87.5 to 88.5 from y 99 to 118 (one bundle of three at 0.4 mm pitch fits it, but its north end at y 98 is closed on F.Cu by the +5V run and the GND vias and on B.Cu by the WT2_SP_RAW diagonal and vertical at x 87.9, y 97.55 to 101.45); U601 pins 18 and 20 are walled on the east by the C612 ground neck and the +3V3 run on y 86.4 to 87.8; the MCU end leaves U401.52 to .54 at x 97 to 98 on the top row, 10.8 mm from the nearest free B.Cu, with the B.Cu +3V3 run at y 11.2 (x 86 to 103.8) below it. The smallest levers: re-site the three GND vias at (88.45, 102.17), (89.75, 102.17) and (87.07, 100.98) to open the north end of the x 88 lane (Ground lock re-run), and give the AMC pair a B.Cu lane west of the MCU along y 8.6 to 9.2 (free from x 69 to 81) with the TP411 run at (88.5, 8.9) to (94.5, 4.75) moved off x 88.5 to 94.5.

Fast class, round 2d-c, the two crystals (unsigned; the Fast class is not locked). `HSE_IN`, `HSE_OUT`, `LSE_IN` and `LSE_OUT` are routed and whole by the independent net check (open pads 267 to 258, Fast split nets 9 to 5, no pad-net mismatch, no net newly split, every new clearance at the class figures, every segment on 0, 45 or 90 degrees and at least 0.3 mm). Each crystal is turned 180 degrees so its pad order follows its pin order, which removes the crossing, and neither pair has a via on the crystal nets except one on `HSE_IN` for its test point. Y401 sits at (92.4, 29.6) (was (90.55, 29.6)): pin 12 reaches the west pad on a 45 degree leg and pin 13 the east pad on a mirrored one, then a straight stub into each pad centre. Y402 sits at (87.0, 28.95) (was (85.2, 28.65)): pin 8 runs the upper lane at y 27.55 to the west pad and pin 9 the lower lane at y 28.0 to the east pad, with no crossing. The four load capacitors lie directly under their crystal pads on straight ties, each with one GND via beside its ground pad: C416 (85.75, 32.325), C417 (88.25, 32.325), C414 (90.55, 33.225) and C415 (94.25, 33.225), the HSE pair one row lower than the LSE pair because the Y401 courtyard ends at y 31.7. C406 moved to (89.45, 36.225) and C401 to (96.0, 36.2), since their old places lie under the new pads; TP406 moved from (95.75, 36.75) to (92.4, 33.3) with a 0.35 mm F.Cu tap and a via at (91.9, 32.45) onto a short B.Cu stub, the one via of the pair (the probe load sits on `HSE_IN` only; `HSE_OUT` has no test point). The +3V3 wall at y 28.0 (x 87.75 to 93.3), the diagonals and stubs of pins 6 and 11, the x 87.3 and x 91.05 legs and the y 29.95 leg east of x 84.8 were removed, so U401 pins 6 and 11 now leave north on 0.2 mm stubs to a jumper along y 23.95 (x 87.05 to 91.5) that meets the north tree at (87.05, 23.95); the east and west halves of +3V3, which the wall had joined, are rejoined by an F.Cu run along y 34.75 (x 93.65 to 87.0), a via at (87.0, 34.75), a B.Cu run west of the locked `+5V_GAUGE` bar (x 87.0 from y 34.75 to 30.3, then west to x 84.4) and a via at (84.4, 30.3) onto the y 29.95 leg. The trunk via at (94.95, 34.65) moved to (95.35, 34.25), still on the same F.Cu diagonal, because the C415 ground pad now lies beside it, and the B.Cu trunk end was re-laid to it. The GND via on U401 pin 10 moved from (91.0, 24.925) to (90.75, 24.75) on a 45 degree stub. Thirteen GND vias at the old capacitor places were removed and six placed beside the new ground pads. Supply3V3 and Ground were re-locked (25 items newly locked). What remains for the fenced router: U401 pins 2 to 5 (`KEY2` to `KEY5`) cannot leave south any more, since the `LSE_IN` lane at y 27.55 runs under them from x 89.7 to 86.55 (the old +3V3 diagonal walled them already); they leave north through vias beside their pads.

Rule fixes after the crystal round, round 2d-c (unsigned; no signed row changes). TP671 stood at (87.9, 123.2) inside the analog sensing region, where the region rule lets only TP672 and TP673 stand (TP672 was removed later, with eleven other test points), and sits now at (100.9, 125.9), inside the digital region whose test points include it; the `WT1_INA_P` tap keeps its F.Cu leg and its 0.6 mm via at (88.8, 122.3) and runs on B.Cu as a 3.6 mm diagonal and an 8.5 mm straight run to the pad (12.1 mm of open stub on the load-cell amplifier's positive input, the price of the region rule; the net stays whole). The +3V3 feeder via of the U621 supply at (80.55, 109.25) lay in the DECOUPLE-6 box of C641.1 and U620.20; it sits now at (84.35, 108.45) on the F.Cu +3V3 run east of C642.1, outside the boxes of C641 and C642, with the B.Cu feeder run re-laid along y 108.85 (x 80.15 to 83.95) between 45 degree legs, `+3V3` still one net, Supply3V3 re-locked (4 items newly locked). The four clearance items 0.2 to 5 micrometres under the 0.2 mm figure are cleared: the `SPAN2_RAW` B.Cu and F.Cu legs at TP532 moved 0.05 mm east with their via at (116.85, 53.8) (now 0.6 mm), R613 moved 0.04 mm to (68.41, 90.41) so its pad 2 clears the locked +3V3 diagonal and the AMC_DOUT_PIN diagonal alike, and the two that touched the crystal area (the +3V3 track against U401 pad 5 and the GND via against R833 pad 1) went with the removal of the +3V3 wall and the old load-capacitor vias. The independent net check is unchanged by these fixes (258 open pads, no mismatch, no net newly split).
